\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\|u_{p,\ell}-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\left(\int_{B(0,2\eta)}r^{2(P-p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}r\,r\mathrm{d}\theta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\eta^{P-p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;\eta^{P-2}\qquad\quad\text{because }\varepsilon^{p}\lesssim\eta^{p}\text{ and }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-2}

2.

\|\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}
:
Similarly,
U_{p,\ell}-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})=o_{\partial}(x^{P-p})
in
\Lambda
when
x\rightarrow\infty
. Thus

•

U_{p,\ell}-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})=O(x^{P-p})

so
U_{p,\ell}(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})=O(x^{P-p})

•

\partial_{x}\big[U_{p,\ell}-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})\big]=O(x^{P-p-1})

so
\partial_{x}\big[U_{p,\ell}(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})\big]=O(\varepsilon^{-1}x^{P-p-1})

•

\partial_{Y}\big[U_{p,\ell}-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})\big]=O(x^{P-p})

so
\partial_{Y}\big[U_{p,\ell}(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})-T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})(x,\frac{y}{\varepsilon})\big]=O(\varepsilon^{-1}x^{P-p})

which are all
O(\varepsilon^{-1}x^{P-p-1})
(uniformly in
Y
). Hence:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\,\left(\int_{[0,2\eta]\times[-\varepsilon,0]}x^{2(P-p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{P-p-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;\eta^{P-2}\qquad\quad\text{because }\varepsilon=\eta^{2}\text{ and }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}

3.

\|S_{\varepsilon,P}-S_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap C_{\eta})}
:
Similarly
S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})=o_{\partial}(r^{p-P})
in
\Omega
when
r\rightarrow\infty
, so
S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})=O(r^{p-P})\text{ and }\nabla\!_{(X,Y)}\big[S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\big]=O(r^{p-P-1})=O(r^{p-P})
uniformly in
\theta
. Since
\nabla\!_{(x,y)}=\varepsilon^{-1}\nabla\!_{(X,Y)}
, we deduce:

\displaystyle\|S_{\varepsilon,P}-S_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\Big\|\big[S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\big]({\textstyle\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}})\Big\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,\eta)}\left(\frac{r}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2(p-P)}\,\mathrm{d}r\,r\mathrm{d}\theta\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{P-p-1}\cdot\eta^{p-P+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;\eta^{P-2}\qquad\quad\text{because }\varepsilon=\eta^{2}\text{ and }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-1}

4.

\|S_{\varepsilon,P}-S_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}
:

S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})=o_{\partial}(X^{p-P})
in
\Lambda
when
x\rightarrow 0
, so:

•

S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})=O(X^{p-P})

•

\partial_{X}\big[S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\big]=O(X^{p-P-1})=O(X^{p-P})

•

\partial_{Y}\big[S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\big]=O(X^{p-P})

uniformy in
Y
. Hence:

\displaystyle\|S_{\varepsilon,P}-S_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,\Big\|\big[S_{p,\ell}-T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\big]({\textstyle\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}})\Big\|_{H^{1}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{-1}\,\left(\int_{[\eta,\infty[\times[-\varepsilon,0]}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2(p-P)}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon^{P-p-1}\cdot\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{p-P+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\;\eta^{P-2}\qquad\quad\text{because }\varepsilon=\eta^{2}\text{ and }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-\frac{3}{2}}

5.

\|\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}-S_{\varepsilon,P}^{\mathcal{A}}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap C_{\eta})}
:
Let us show that this norm vanishes. It suffices to prove that in
\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\Pi)
:

{}\fsum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\fsum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\epsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\epsilon\cdot T_{\leqslant P-p}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\bigg(\fsum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\fsum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\epsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\epsilon\cdot T_{\geqslant p-P}(S^{\infty}_{p,\ell})\bigg)

(4.1)

where
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}
is defined in (
2.30
)–(
2.32
), and
\epsilon
and
\ln\epsilon
denote the algebraic indeterminates of
\mathcal{A}^{+}_{\varepsilon}(\Pi)
(we denote them differently from Sections
2.1
and
2.5
to avoid confusion with the real number
\varepsilon
). For any
(p,d)
let
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}
be the coordinate of
\fsum_{p,\ell}\epsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\epsilon\,\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell}
in
\mathcal{A}_{d}(\Pi)[\ln\epsilon]
and
\tilde{S}_{p,d}
be the one of
\fsum_{p,\ell}\epsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\epsilon\,S^{\infty}_{p,\ell}
. Moreover, for any
d
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{A}_{d}(\Pi)[\ln\epsilon]
, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\varphi):=\varepsilon^{d}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\varphi)\in\mathcal{A}_{d}(\Pi)[\ln\epsilon]
. Then:

(
4.1
)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\fsum_{d\leqslant P-p}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\bigg(\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\fsum_{d\geqslant p-P}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{S}_{p,d}\bigg)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\fsum_{p+d\leqslant P}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\fsum_{p-d\leqslant P}\epsilon^{p-d}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\tilde{S}_{p,d})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\fsum_{p+d\leqslant P}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\fsum_{p+d\leqslant P}\fsum_{p\leqslant P}\epsilon^{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\tilde{S}_{p+d,d})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\forall(p,d)\text{ s.t. }p\leqslant P\text{ and }p+d\leqslant P,\quad\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\tilde{S}_{p+d,d})

(4.2)

because two formal series coincide iff their coordinates coincide one by one.

But Proposition
3.3
shows the matching condition
\fsum\limits_{p,d}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\Big(\fsum\limits_{p,d}\epsilon^{p}\tilde{S}_{p,d}\Big)=\fsum\limits_{p,d}\epsilon^{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\tilde{S}_{p+d,d})
, which is equivalent to:
\forall(p,d),\;\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{p,d}=\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{-1}(\tilde{S}_{p+d,d})
. Thus we get (
4.2
), and then (
4.1
).

\scriptstyle\Box

Theorem 4.2: global error estimate

For any
P\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
we have
\big\|u_{\varepsilon}-u_{\varepsilon,P}\big\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}=o(\varepsilon^{\frac{P}{2}-2})
.

.

Proof:
Let
r_{\varepsilon,P}:=u_{\varepsilon,P}-u_{\varepsilon}
. It satisfies, for some functions
f_{\varepsilon}
and
g_{\varepsilon}
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\;=\;}ll}\operatorname{div}(\mu_{\varepsilon}\nabla r_{\varepsilon,P})+\omega^{2}\rho_{\varepsilon}r_{\varepsilon,P}&f_{\varepsilon}&\text{ in }\Omega_{\varepsilon}\\[1.42262pt]
r_{\varepsilon,P}&0&\text{ on }\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}\\[1.42262pt]
r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{+}}-r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{-}}&0&\text{ on }\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\}\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{0}\partial_{y}r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{y}r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{-}}&g_{\varepsilon}&\text{ on }\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\}\end{array}\right.

As for (
1.2
), this problem is well-posed with a stability constant independent of
\varepsilon
:

\|r_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}+\|g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\}).}

To get our error estimate, it suffices to show that
\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{P}{2}-2}=\eta^{P-4}
.

Estimate of
\|f_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}
:
We denote:

•

\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}:=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}_{p,\ell}
in
\Pi_{\varepsilon}

•

S_{\varepsilon,P}:=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,S_{p,\ell}({\textstyle\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}})
in
\Omega_{\varepsilon}

•

and
\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}:u\mapsto\operatorname{div}(\mu_{\varepsilon}\nabla u)+\omega^{2}\rho_{\varepsilon}u
the differential operator of Helmholtz’s equation.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}u_{\varepsilon}=f
by definition of
u_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}r_{\varepsilon,P}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\big((1-\chi_{\eta})\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}+\chi_{\eta}S_{\varepsilon,P}-u_{\varepsilon}\big)

\displaystyle=\big((1-\chi_{\eta})\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}-f\big)+\chi_{\eta}\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}S_{\varepsilon,P}+[\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon},\chi_{\eta}](S_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P})

where
[.,.]
is the commutator. Let us estimate these terms one by one.

1.

For
\varepsilon
small enough,
1-\chi_{\eta}
is equal to 0 in
B(0,\varepsilon R_{\mathrm{c}})
and 1 in
\operatorname{supp}(f)
, so
(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P|\Omega}=(1-\chi_{\eta})(\mu_{0}\Delta+\omega^{2}\rho_{0})\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P|\Omega}=(1-\chi_{\eta})f=f
.

Moreover, using that
\mu_{1}\partial_{Y}^{2}U_{p,\ell}=-(\mu_{1}\partial_{x}^{2}+\omega^{2}\rho_{1})U_{p-2,\ell}
for any
(p,\ell)
, we get in
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\setminus B(0,\varepsilon R_{\mathrm{c}})
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}

\displaystyle=(\mu_{1}\Delta+\omega^{2}\rho_{1})\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,(\mu_{1}\partial_{x}^{2}+\omega^{2}\rho_{1})U_{p,\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p+\frac{1}{2}}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{1}\,(\partial_{x}^{2}+k_{1}^{2})^{n+1}\partial_{y}u_{p-1-2n,\ell}(x,0)\cdot\mathcal{U}_{n}({\textstyle\frac{y}{\varepsilon}})

by (
•
‣
3.3
)

Since
\mathcal{U}_{n}\in L^{\infty}(-1,0)
for any
n
(see (
3.1
)), we deduce:

\|(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{L^{2}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\!\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor{(p-1)/2}\rfloor}\varepsilon^{p+\frac{1}{2}}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\big\|(\partial_{x}^{2}+k_{1}^{2})^{n+1}\partial_{y}u_{p-1-2n,\ell}\big\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\})}.

Let us show that, for any
p,\ell,m
,
\|\partial_{x}^{m}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\})}\lesssim\eta^{-p-m-1}
(4.3).

•

The proof of Proposition
3.3
shows that
\partial_{x}^{m}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell}\in H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma)+\chi_{0}\mathcal{A}(\Gamma)
for any
p,\ell,m
. So
\|\partial_{x}^{m}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>1\})}<\infty
.

•

By Proposition
3.3
,
\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}=\partial_{y}T_{\leqslant-p-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})+o_{\partial}(x^{-p-\frac{3}{2}})
when
x\rightarrow 0
, and Ansatz
2.5
is satisfied, thus
T_{\leqslant-p-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{0}_{p,\ell})=0
. So:
\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\;\partial_{x}^{m}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}=O(x^{-p-\frac{3}{2}-m})
when
x\rightarrow 0
. It implies
\|\partial_{x}^{m}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{\eta<x<1\})}\lesssim\eta^{-p-m-1}
.

Hence:

\displaystyle\|(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{L^{2}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p+\frac{1}{2}}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor{(p-1)/2}\rfloor}\eta^{-(p-1-2n)-2(n+1)-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p+\frac{1}{2}}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\eta^{-p-2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{P-4}\qquad\quad\text{because }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-1}

So
\|(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}-f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\eta^{P-4}
.

2.

For the second term of
f_{\varepsilon}
, (
3.5
) implies that
\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}S_{\varepsilon,P}=\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,\omega^{2}\rho S_{p,\ell}
. But by Proposition
3.3
and Ansatz
2.5
:
\forall(p,\ell),\;S_{p,\ell}=O(r^{p})
. Thus, using that
\rho
is bounded and
\|S_{p,\ell}(\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap B(0,2\eta))}=\varepsilon\,\|S_{p,\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{1}\cap B(0,2\eta/\varepsilon))}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\chi_{\eta}\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon}S_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\varepsilon\,\|S_{p,\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{1}\cap B(0,2\eta/\varepsilon))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-2,P]}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n_{p}}\varepsilon^{p+1}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\Big(\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{p+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{P-4}\qquad\quad\text{because }\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-3}

3.

Using that
\|\Delta\chi_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\chi_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}=O(\eta^{-2})
, the last term of
f_{\varepsilon}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{D}_{\varepsilon},\chi_{\eta}](S_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta\chi_{\eta}\cdot(S_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P})+2\nabla\chi_{\eta}\cdot\nabla(S_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{-2}\|S_{\varepsilon,P}-\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap B(0,2\eta)\setminus B(0,\eta))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta^{P-4}\qquad\quad\text{by Lemma\penalty\ \ref{erreur de raccord}.}

Estimate of
\|g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}
:
Without loss of generality, we assume
\partial_{y}\chi_{\eta|\Gamma}=0
. (
3.5
) implies that
\mu_{0}\partial_{Y}S_{p,\ell|Y=0^{+}}=\mu_{1}\partial_{Y}S_{p,\ell|Y=0^{-}}
on
\Gamma\cap\{X>R_{\mathrm{c}}\}
for any
(p,\ell)
. Thus we have on
\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\}
:

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\mu_{0}\partial_{y}r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{y}r_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{-}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{\eta}\cdot\big(\mu_{0}\partial_{y}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{y}\mathbf{u}_{\varepsilon,P|y=0^{-}}\big)

\displaystyle=\chi_{\eta}\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P-1,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\mu_{0}\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|y=0^{+}}

By (
1
), we have that
\|\partial_{y}u_{p,\ell|\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\})}\lesssim\eta^{-p-1}
. So
\|g_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap\{x>\eta\})}\lesssim\eta^{P-4}
since
\ln^{\ell}\varepsilon\lesssim\eta^{-2}
.

\scriptstyle\Box

Remark:

Theorem
4
can be improved to
\|u_{\varepsilon}-u_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}=o(\varepsilon^{P/2})
. Indeed with the same notations as the above proof, one can show that, for any
(p,\ell)\in\mathbb{P}\times\mathbb{N}
,
\|(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}_{p,\ell}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\eta^{-p}
and
\|\chi_{\eta}S_{p,\ell}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\eta^{-p}
. Therefore:

\|r_{\varepsilon,P}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\|r_{\varepsilon,P+4}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}+\!\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P,P+4]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\cdot\big(\|(1-\chi_{\eta})\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}_{p,\ell}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}+\|\chi_{\eta}S_{p,\ell}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\big)=o(\varepsilon^{\frac{P}{2}}).

.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
:

It follows from Theorem
4
applied at order
2P+4
and from the fact that, for small enough
\varepsilon
,
\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,2P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,u_{p,\ell}
and
u_{\varepsilon,2P}
coincide in
\Omega\setminus B(0,\delta)
:

\displaystyle\bigg\|u_{\varepsilon}-\!\!\!\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,P]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\!\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,u_{p,\ell}\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\setminus B(0,\delta))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{\varepsilon}-u_{\varepsilon,2P+4}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}+\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap(P,2P+4]}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\!\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,\|u_{p,\ell}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\setminus B(0,\delta))}

\displaystyle=o(\varepsilon^{P})+o(\varepsilon^{P}).

\scriptstyle\Box

Remark:

An alternative way of stating the error estimate is:

\bigg\|u_{\varepsilon}-\sum_{p\in\mathbb{P}\cap[0,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}P})}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\varepsilon^{p}\ln^{\ell}\!\varepsilon\,u_{p,\ell}\bigg\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\setminus B(0,\delta))}=O(\varepsilon^{P}\ln^{n}\!\varepsilon)\qquad\text{with}\ n:=\max\{\ell\in\mathbb{N}\mid u_{P,\ell}\neq 0\}.

Appendix A
Appendix: proof of Lemma
2.2

.

By Definition
2.2
, we already know that the formulas to prove are true replacing
\bigoplus
by
\sum
. So it suffices to show that those sums are direct.

1.

Let us show that
\mathcal{A}(\Omega)=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{A}_{d}(\Omega)
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and, for any
j\in[\![1,n]\!]
,
d_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varphi_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{d_{j}}(\Omega)
. We assume that
d_{1}<d_{2}<\cdots<d_{n}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}=0
. Let us show that
\forall j\in[\![1,n]\!],\;\varphi_{j}=0
.
Let
\theta\in(0,\Theta)
. By definition of
\mathcal{A}_{d_{j}}(\Omega)
, for any
j\in[\![1,n]\!]
, there is
P_{\theta,j}\in\mathbb{C}[T]
s.t.:
\forall r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\;\varphi_{j}(r,\theta)=r^{d_{j}}P_{\theta,j}(\ln r)
. Let us assume by contradiction that:
\exists j\in[\![1,n]\!],\;P_{\theta,j}\neq 0
. Let
j_{0}:=\max\{j\in[\![1,n]\!]\mid P_{\theta,j}\neq 0\}
. Then
0=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}\varphi_{i}(r,\theta)\sim r^{d_{j_{0}}}P_{\theta,j_{0}}(\ln r)
when
r\rightarrow\infty
, so
P_{\theta,j_{0}}=0
. This is contradictory, so:
\forall\theta\in(0,\Theta),\forall j\in[\![1,n]\!],\forall r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*},\;\varphi_{j}(r,\theta)=0
.

2.

By the same method, one can show that
\mathcal{A}(D)=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{A}_{d}(D)
for any
D\in\{\Pi,\Gamma,\Lambda\}
.

3.

Now we will prove (
2.7
). Let
d\in\mathbb{R}
and
I:=\{(q,k)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}\mid q+k=d\}
. Separating real and imaginary parts, it is enough to show that:

\mathcal{A}_{d}(\Omega)\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})=\bigoplus_{(q,k)\in I}\big\{z\mapsto\Im[(\alpha z)^{q}\,\overline{\alpha z}^{k}P(\log(\alpha z))]\ \big|\ P\in\mathbb{R}[T]\text{ and }\mathcal{P}(q,k,P)\big\}.

Let, for any
(q,k)\in I
,
P_{q,k}\in\mathbb{R}[T]
and
\varphi_{q,k}:z\mapsto\Im[(\alpha z)^{q}\,\overline{\alpha z}^{k}P_{q,k}(\log(\alpha z))]
. We assume that
\mathcal{P}(q,k,P_{q,k})
holds for any
(q,k)\in I
, that the
P_{q,k}
are all null except for a finite number, and that
\sum_{(q,k)\in I}\varphi_{q,k}=0
. Let us show by induction on
m:=\max_{(q,k)\in I}\deg P_{q,k}
that:
\forall(q,k)\in I,\;P_{q,k}=0
(which implies in turn:
\forall(q,k),\;\varphi_{q,k}=0
). We initialize at
m=-\infty
, i.e.
(\forall(q,k),\;P_{q,k}=0)
, which is trivial. Thus, only the inductive step (
m\in\mathbb{N}
) remains to prove.

To do so, we first note that in
\Omega
:

{}0=r^{-d}\sum_{(q,k)\in I}\varphi_{q,k}(r,\theta)=\sum_{(q,k)\in I}\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}(\ln r+\mathrm{i}(\theta-\Theta))]

(A.1)

By applying
r\partial_{r}
, we deduce:
\displaystyle 0=\sum_{(q,k)\in I}\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}^{\prime}(\ln r+\mathrm{i}(\theta-\Theta))]
.

For any
(q,k)\in I
, let us define (note the switch of indexes at line 2):

Q_{q,k}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}P_{q,k}^{\prime}&\text{if }q\not\in\mathbb{N}\\[1.42262pt]
P_{q,k}^{\prime}-P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)&\text{if }q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q\leqslant k\\[1.42262pt]
P_{q,k}^{\prime}-P_{k,q}^{\prime}(0)&\text{if }q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q>k\end{array}\right.

Then
(\forall(q,k)\in I,\;\mathcal{P}(q,k,Q_{q,k}))
. Since
\Im[(\alpha z)^{q}\,\overline{\alpha z}^{k}]=-\Im[(\alpha z)^{k}\,\overline{\alpha z}^{q}]
(and it is null when
q=k
), the previous equality rewrites as

0=\sum_{(q,k)\in I}\Im[(\alpha z)^{q}\,\overline{\alpha z}^{k}Q_{q,k}(\log(\alpha z))].

Then, by induction hypothesis:
\forall(q,k)\in I,\;Q_{q,k}=0
. This means for
P_{q,k}
that:

•

If
q\not\in\mathbb{N}
, then
P_{q,k}^{\prime}=0
.

•

If
q\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\neq k
, then
P_{q,k}^{\prime\prime}=0
and
P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)-P_{k,q}^{\prime}(0)=0
(A.2).

•

If
q\in\mathbb{N}
and
q=k
, then
P_{q,k}^{\prime\prime}=0
.

So (
A.1
) reduces to :

\begin{array}[]{r@{\;}r@{}l@{\hspace{-10ex}}l}0&=\displaystyle\sum_{(q,k)\in I}&\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}(0)]+\mathbf{1}_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\cdot\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)(\ln r+\mathrm{i}(\theta-\Theta))]\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\\[7.11317pt]
&=\displaystyle\sum_{(q,k)\in I}&\mathbf{1}_{(q\not\in\mathbb{N}\text{ or }q>k)}\cdot\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}(0)]\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\text{by }\mathcal{P}\\
&+&\mathbf{1}_{(q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q=k)}\cdot\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)(\ln r+\mathrm{i}(\theta-\Theta))]\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\\[5.69054pt]
&+&\mathbf{1}_{(q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q>k)}\cdot\Im[e^{\mathrm{i}(q-k)(\theta-\Theta)}P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)\,2\mathrm{i}(\theta-\Theta)]\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\text{by \eqref{eq: preuve decomposition de A eq2}}\\[7.11317pt]
&=\displaystyle\sum_{(q,k)\in I}&\mathbf{1}_{(q\not\in\mathbb{N}\text{ or }q>k)}\cdot P_{q,k}(0)\cdot\sin((q-k)(\theta-\Theta))\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\\
&+&\mathbf{1}_{(q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q=k)}\cdot P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)\cdot(\theta-\Theta)\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\\[5.69054pt]
&+&\mathbf{1}_{(q\in\mathbb{N}\text{ and }q>k)}\cdot 2P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)\cdot\sin\!\big((q-k)(\theta-\Theta)+\frac{\pi}{2}\big)\cdot(\theta-\Theta)\hfil\hskip-43.05542pt&\end{array}

The functions of
\theta
present here are linearly independent. So the coefficients
P_{q,k}(0)
and
P_{q,k}^{\prime}(0)
are all zero. This concludes the proof.

\scriptstyle\Box

Appendix B

\mathbf{r}

2.4
and
2.4
. We will use Sections
3.1
and
3.2
which are after Theorems
2.4
and
2.4
in the paper, but are independent of them.

B.1
Proof of Theorem
2.4
: asymptotic behavior for corner fields-like problems

The proof relies on the Kondrat’ev theory, usually used to analyse singularities of solutions of elliptic equations, see
[

,

,

,

.

First we introduce the variables

in
\Omega
and
(\ln x,Y)
in
\Lambda
. The pair

. Moreover, we denote

the images of
\Omega
,
\Lambda
,
\Sigma_{\Omega}
,
\Gamma
and
\Sigma_{\Lambda}
by the change of variable
(x,y)\rightsquigarrow(t,\theta)
, see Figure

. The notation
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.45831pt]{\ldots}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\raisebox{0.0pt}[6.45831pt]{\ldots}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\raisebox{0.0pt}[4.52083pt]{\ldots}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\raisebox{0.0pt}[4.52083pt]{\ldots}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
is intended to remind the strip shape of
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Pi$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Pi$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Pi$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Pi$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}

\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}:\,(t,\theta)\in\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Pi$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Pi$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Pi$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Pi$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\;\mapsto\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u(r=e^{t},\theta)&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Omega$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Omega$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
u(x=e^{t},Y=\theta)&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\end{array}\right.

Figure 10:
The change of variables
(x,y)\rightsquigarrow(t,\theta)
and the associated domains.

Definition B.1: Kondrat’ev spaces

Let
H
be a Hilbert space and
(s,\beta)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We define
K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H):=\{t\mapsto e^{\beta t}u(t)\mid u\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R},H)\}
, equipped with the norm
\|u\|_{K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H)}:=\|t\mapsto e^{-\beta t}u(t)\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R},H)}
. We also denote
K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H):=\bigcap_{s\in\mathbb{R}}K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H)
, and
K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}):=K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{C})
(identifying the line
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
with
\mathbb{R}
).

Remarks:

•

If
s=m\in\mathbb{N}
, then
K^{m}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H)=\{u\in H^{m}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R},H)\mid\forall k\in[\![0,m]\!],\;e^{-\beta t}\partial_{t}^{k}u(t)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R},H)\}
.

•

Note that if
u\in K^{s}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H)
and
u
is zero in a vicinity of
-\infty
, then:
\forall\beta^{\prime}\geqslant\beta,\;u\in K^{s}_{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R},H)
.

•

Kondrat’ev’s spaces are linked to
o_{\partial}
(see Definition
2.4
) in the following way. Let
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be equal to 0 in a vicinity of
-\infty
and 1 in a vicinity of
+\infty
. Then for any
u:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad\;}r@{\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;}l}\forall\beta\in\mathbb{R},&\chi\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\in\!\smash{\bigcap\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}}K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H^{m}(0,\Theta))\;\;\mathrel{\mbox{$=\hskip-6.5pt\Rightarrow$}}&\forall d>\beta,\;u\!\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{=}\!o_{\partial}(r^{d})\\[1.42262pt]
\forall d\in\mathbb{R},&u\!\underset{r\rightarrow\infty}{=}\!o_{\partial}(r^{d})\;\;\mathrel{\mbox{$=\hskip-6.5pt\Rightarrow$}}&\exists t_{0}\in\mathbb{R},\,\forall\beta>d,\ \;\chi(\cdot+t_{0})\,\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\in\!\smash{\bigcap\limits_{m\in\mathbb{N}}}K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H^{m}(0,\Theta))\end{array}\right.

And there are similar implications in
\Lambda
and
\Gamma
.

Let
H
be a Hilbert space,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varphi\in K^{0}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H)
. For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
s.t.
\Re(\lambda)=\beta
we define the (bilateral) Laplace transform of
\varphi
at
\lambda
as

{}\widehat{\varphi}(\lambda):=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\lambda t}\varphi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\mathcal{F}\big[t\mapsto e^{-\beta t}\varphi(t)\big](\Im(\lambda))

(B.1)

where
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Fourier transform. By properties of
\mathcal{F}
, we have
\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{2}(\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid\Re(\lambda)=\beta\})
. If
\varphi
depends on
t
and
\theta
,
\widehat{\varphi}
implies that we see
\varphi
as a function from
\mathbb{R}
to a space of functions of
\theta
.

To introduce the method, let us use the Laplace transform on
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle S\,$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle S\,$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle S\,$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle S\,$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
​. Let
\chi_{\infty}
be the truncation function introduced in Section
3.2
and
s:=\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
. Since
S\in V+\chi_{\infty}\mathcal{A}(\Pi)
, there is
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.
s\in K^{0}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},L^{2}(-1,\Theta))
, so
\hat{s}(\lambda,\theta)
is well-defined for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
s.t.
\Re(\lambda)>\beta
. In addition we have for any
f\in L^{2}(\Omega)
,
g\in L^{2}(\Lambda)
,
h\in H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)
and
u\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Pi)
:

{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}r@{\;\;}l@{}}-\mu_{0}\Delta u=f&\text{ in }\Omega\\[1.42262pt]
-\mu_{1}\Delta u=g&\text{ in }\Lambda\\[1.42262pt]
u_{|y=0^{+}}-u_{|y=0^{-}}=0&\text{ on }\Gamma\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{0}\partial_{y}u_{|y=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{y}u_{|y=0^{-}}=h&\text{ on }\Gamma\\[1.42262pt]
u=0&\text{ on }\Sigma_{\Omega}\cup\Sigma_{\Lambda}\end{array}\right.\;\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}r@{\;\;}l@{}}-e^{-2t}\mu_{0}\Delta\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}=\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle f$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle f$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle f$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle f$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Omega$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern -\mu_{1}\,(e^{-2t}(\partial_{t}^{2}-\partial_{t})+\partial_{\theta}^{2})\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}=\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle g$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle g$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle g$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle g$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}_{|\theta=0^{+}}-\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}_{|\theta=0^{-}}=0&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
e^{-t}\mu_{0}\partial_{\theta}\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}_{|\theta=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{\theta}\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}_{|\theta=0^{-}}=\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle h$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle h$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle h$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle h$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle u$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle u$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}=0&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\cup\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\end{array}\right.

(B.2)

Taking
u:=\chi_{\infty}S
in (
B.2
) and applying the Laplace transform yield that
\hat{s}
satisfies:

{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\;=\;}ll}(\partial_{\theta}^{2}+\lambda^{2})\hat{s}(\lambda,\theta)&f&\text{if }\theta\in(0,\Theta)\\[1.42262pt]
\partial_{\theta}^{2}\hat{s}(\lambda,\theta)&g-\big[(\lambda+2)^{2}-(\lambda+2)\big]\,\hat{s}(\lambda+2,\theta)&\text{if }\theta\in(-1,0)\\[1.42262pt]
\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{+})-\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{-})&0&\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{1}\partial_{\theta}\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{-})&\mu_{0}\partial_{\theta}\hat{s}(\lambda+1,0^{+})&\\[1.42262pt]
\hat{s}(\lambda,\Theta)=\hat{s}(\lambda,-1)&0&\end{array}\right.

(B.3)

for some functions functions
f
and
g
depending on
F
and
\chi_{\infty}
. Solving this system w.r.t.
\theta
allows us to extend
\hat{s}
w.r.t.
\lambda
further to the left in the complex plane, except at the
\lambda
for which (
B.3
) is ill-posed. These
\lambda
are poles of
\hat{s}
and they will be used in Proposition
B.1
to identify terms of the asymptotic expansion of
S
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, let us define the Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}^{m}:=\{u\in H^{1}_{0}(-1,\Theta)\mid u_{|(0,\Theta)}\in H^{m}(0,\Theta)\text{ and }u_{|(-1,0)}\in H^{m}(-1,0)\}
with the norm
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}^{2}:=\|u\|_{H^{m}(-1,0)}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{m}(0,\Theta)}^{2}
, and
\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}:=H^{m}(0,\Theta)\times H^{m}(-1,0)\times\mathbb{C}
. We need to solve problems of the following form with
u\in\mathcal{H}^{m+2}
and
(f,g,a)\in\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
:

{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\;=\;}ll}u^{\prime\prime}+\lambda^{2}u&f&\text{ on }(0,\Theta)\\[1.42262pt]
u^{\prime\prime}&g&\text{ on }(-1,0)\\[1.42262pt]
u(0^{+})-u(0^{-})&0&\\[1.42262pt]
u^{\prime}(0^{-})&a&\\[1.42262pt]
u(\Theta)=u(-1)&0&\end{array}\right.

(B.4)

Denoting
A(\lambda):u\mapsto\big((u^{\prime\prime}+\lambda^{2}u)_{|(0,\Theta)},\,u^{\prime\prime}_{|(-1,0)},\,u^{\prime}(0^{-})\big)
, (
B.4
) is equivalent to
A(\lambda)(u)=(f,g,a)
. Note that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
A(\lambda)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}^{m+2},\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times})
where
\mathcal{L}({\dots})
denotes the space of continuous linear maps between two normed vector spaces.

Lemma B.2:

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

1.

A(\lambda):\mathcal{H}^{m+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
is invertible iff
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.

2.

\lambda\mapsto A(\lambda)^{-1}
is meromorphic from
\mathbb{C}
to
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times},\mathcal{H}^{m+2})
. Its poles are in
\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
and are simple. Moreover, for any
q\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
and
v\in\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
, the residue
(\operatorname{Res}_{\lambda=q}A(\lambda)^{-1})(v)
is proportional to the function
\theta\in[-1,\Theta]\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{[0,\Theta]}(\theta)\cdot\sin(q\theta)
.

3.

Let
\beta_{1}<\beta_{2}
be some reals. There is
C>0
depending only on
(m,\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
s.t., for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
satisfying
\beta_{1}<\Re(\lambda)<\beta_{2}
and
|\Im(\lambda)|>1
, we have:
\|A(\lambda)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times},\mathcal{H}^{m+2})}\leqslant C\,|\Im(\lambda)|^{m+2}
(B.5).

.

Proof:

1.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
. An easy calculation gives that any element of
\operatorname{Ker}A(\lambda)
must be proportional to
\theta\in[-1,\Theta]\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{[0,\Theta]}(\theta)\cdot\sin(\lambda\theta)
. This function belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{m}\setminus\{0\}
iff
\lambda\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
. Therefore
\operatorname{Ker}A(\lambda)\neq 0\mathrel{\mbox{$\Leftarrow\hskip-6.0pt\Rightarrow$}}\lambda\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.

Moreover, it is easy to see that, for any
(f,g,a)\in\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
, (
B.4
) with
\lambda:=0
has a unique solution in
\mathcal{H}^{m+2}
. Thus
A(0):\mathcal{H}^{m+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
is invertible. Now, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, we have
A(\lambda)=A(0)+\lambda^{2}B
with
B:u\mapsto(u_{|(0,\Theta)},0,0)
.
B
is a compact operator from
\mathcal{H}^{m+2}
to
\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
, so the Fredholm alternative holds for
A(\lambda)
. Therefore
A(\lambda):\mathcal{H}^{m+2}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
is invertible iff
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.

2.

Since
\lambda\mapsto A(\lambda)
is holomorphic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
, so too is
\lambda\mapsto A(\lambda)^{-1}
. Let us describe its behavior near the points
q\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
using
[
31
, Theorem 5.1.1, p.147]
. It depends on the “Jordan chains” of
A(q)
, which are the sequences
(u_{0},\dots,u_{n})\in(\mathcal{H}^{m})^{n+1}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, s.t.

{}\forall k\in[\![0,n]\!],\qquad\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{1}{j!}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{j}A}{\mathrm{d}\lambda^{j}}\Big|_{\lambda=q}(u_{k-j})=0

(B.6)

Let us compute these chains. Taking
k:=0
in (
B.6
) gives
A(q)(u_{0})=0
, so
u_{0}
is proportional to
\theta\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{[0,\Theta]}(\theta)\cdot\sin(q\theta)
by step 1. In addition, if
n\geqslant 1
, taking
k:=1
gives
A(q)(u_{1})+\frac{\mathrm{d}A}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}(q)(u_{0})=0
. This implies on one hand
u_{1}^{\prime\prime}+q^{2}u_{1}+2qu_{0}=0
in
(0,\Theta)
. On the other we get
u_{1|(-1,0)}^{\prime\prime}=0
and
u_{1}^{\prime}(0^{-})=0
, so
u_{1|(-1,0)}=0
, which gives
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(\Theta)=0
. Therefore

0\neq\int_{0}^{\Theta}2q|u_{0}|^{2}=\int_{0}^{\Theta}-(u_{1}^{\prime\prime}+q^{2}u_{1})\cdot\overline{u_{0}}=\int_{0}^{\Theta}-u_{1}\cdot(\underbrace{\overline{u_{0}^{\prime\prime}+q^{2}u_{0}}}_{=0})=0.\vskip-5.69054pt

This is absurd, so we must have
n=0
for any Jordan chain of
A(q)
. Therefore,
[
31
, Theorem 5.1.1, p.147]
states that
A(\lambda)^{-1}
has a simple pole at
q
, and
\operatorname{\operatorname{Im}}(\operatorname{Res}_{\lambda=q}A(\lambda)^{-1})=\mathbb{C}u_{0}
.

3.

Let
(f,g,a)\in\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}
and
u:=A(\lambda)^{-1}(f,g,a)\in\mathcal{H}^{m+2}
. We will write
\lesssim
for inequalities valid up to a constant that depends on
(m,\beta_{1},\beta_{2})
but not on
(\lambda,f,g,a)
. By Poincaré’s inequality and integration by parts, we have

\|u\|_{H^{1}(-1,0)}^{2}\lesssim\int_{-1}^{0}|u^{\prime}|^{2}=-\int_{0}^{-1}g\bar{u}+a\,\bar{u}(0)\lesssim(\|g\|_{L^{2}(-1,0)}+|a|)\cdot\|u\|_{H^{1}(-1,0)}

thus
\|u\|_{H^{1}(-1,0)}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}(-1,0)}+|a|
. Since
u^{\prime\prime}_{|(-1,0)}=g
, we deduce
\|u\|_{H^{m+2}(-1,0)}\lesssim\|g\|_{H^{m}}+|a|
.

Then, let
u_{1}:\theta\in[0,\Theta]\mapsto u(0)\big(1-\frac{\theta}{\Theta}\big)
,
v:=u-u_{1}
and
f_{\lambda}:=f-\lambda^{2}u_{1}
. We have
v(0)=v(\Theta)=0
and
v^{\prime\prime}+\lambda^{2}v=f_{\lambda}
, so

\int_{0}^{\Theta}(-|v^{\prime}|^{2}+\lambda^{2}|v|^{2})=\int_{0}^{\Theta}f_{\lambda}\bar{v}

Dividing by
\lambda
and taking the absolute value of the imaginary part, we get:
\frac{|\Im(\lambda)|}{|\lambda|^{2}}\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\Im(\lambda)|\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{|\lambda|}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{L^{2}}
. Now we asume
|\Im(\lambda)|>1
and
\beta_{1}<\Re(\lambda)<\beta_{2}
, so
|\lambda|\lesssim|\Im(\lambda)|
. Thus
\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+|\lambda|^{2}\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leqslant\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}\|v\|_{L^{2}}
, which yields
\|v\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|\lambda|^{-2}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}
and then
\|v^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim|\lambda|^{-1}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}}
. Now from
v^{\prime\prime}=f_{\lambda}-\lambda^{2}v
, one can easily derive by induction on
m
that
\|v\|_{H^{m+2}}\lesssim|\lambda|^{m}\|f_{\lambda}\|_{H^{m}}
. This implies
\|u\|_{H^{m+2}(0,\Theta)}\lesssim|\lambda|^{m}\|f\|_{H^{m}}+|\lambda|^{m+2}|u(0)|
, with
|u(0)|\lesssim\|u\|_{H^{m+2}(-1,0)}
.

So finally
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m+2}}\lesssim|\Im(\lambda)|^{m+2}\|(f,g,a)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}_{\times}}
.

\scriptstyle\Box

Proposition
B.1
is the base step of the asymptotic expansion. It will be applied to
s
the rest of the asymptotic expansion at a given order, and the function
s_{\mathrm{r}}
below will be the rest at the next order. Iterating this process provides an asymptotic expansion of
S
at any order. Since Theorem
2.4
implies infinite regularity on
S
, we work in the spaces
K^{\infty}_{\beta}
and
\mathcal{H}^{m}
for any
m
.

Proposition B.3:
Let
d\in\mathbb{R}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}
,
s\in\bigcap\limits_{\beta>d}K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{H}^{m})
,
f\in\!\bigcap\limits_{\beta>d-1}\!K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H^{m-2}(0,\Theta))
,
g\in\!\bigcap\limits_{\beta>d-1}\!K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},H^{m-2}(-1,0))
,
h\in\!\bigcap\limits_{\beta>d-1}\!K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu})
and
d_{\mathrm{\min}}:=\min(\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}\cap(d-1,d])
. We assume:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rl}\mu_{0}\Delta s=f&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Omega$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Omega$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Omega$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{1}\,(e^{-2t}(\partial_{t}^{2}-\partial_{t})+\partial_{\theta}^{2})s=g&\text{ in }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Lambda$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
s_{|\theta=0^{+}}-s_{|\theta=0^{-}}=0&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
e^{-t}\mu_{0}\partial_{\theta}s_{|\theta=0^{+}}-\mu_{1}\partial_{\theta}s_{|\theta=0^{-}}=h&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Gamma$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\\[1.42262pt]
s=0&\text{ on }\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Omega}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\cup\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\Sigma_{\Lambda}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\end{array}\right.

Then there is
s_{\mathrm{r}}\in\!\bigcap\limits_{d-1<\beta<d_{\mathrm{\min}}}\!K^{\infty}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{H}^{m})
and
(c_{q})\in\mathbb{C}^{\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}\cap(d-1,d]}
s.t.
\displaystyle s=\sum_{q\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}\cap(d-1,d]}c_{q}\,\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}+s_{\mathrm{r}}
.

.

Proof:
For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>\beta}:=\{\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\mid\Re(\lambda)>\beta\}
. The Laplace transform of
s
is well-defined and holomorphic from
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d}
to
\mathcal{H}^{m}
. We will show that
\hat{s}
has a meromorphic extension on
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d-1}
, with poles belonging in
\mathfrak{P}:=\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}\cap(d-1,d]
, then we will apply the residue theorem on a rectangle surrounding these poles. The functions
\phi_{q}
will appear in the residues.

Step 1 :

\hat{s}
satisfies in
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d}
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{}ll}\mu_{0}(\partial_{\theta}^{2}+\lambda^{2})\hat{s}(\lambda,\theta)&\;=\;\hat{f}(\lambda,\theta)&\text{if}\ \theta\in(0,\Theta)\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{1}\partial_{\theta}^{2}\hat{s}(\lambda,\theta)&\;=\;\hat{g}(\lambda,\theta)-\mu_{1}\,\big[(\lambda+2)^{2}-(\lambda+2)\big]\,\hat{s}(\lambda+2,\theta):=\tilde{g}(\lambda,\theta)&\text{if}\ \theta\in(-1,0)\\[1.42262pt]
\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{+})-\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{-})&\;=\;0&\\[1.42262pt]
\mu_{1}\partial_{\theta}\hat{s}(\lambda,0^{-})&\;=\;\hat{h}(\lambda)+\mu_{0}\partial_{\theta}\hat{s}(\lambda+1,0^{+}):=\tilde{h}(\lambda)&\\[1.42262pt]
\hat{s}(\lambda,\Theta)=\hat{s}(\lambda,-1)&\;=\;0&\end{array}\right.

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d-1}\setminus\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
, let
\tilde{s}(\lambda):=A(\lambda)^{-1}\big(\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}(\lambda),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{g}(\lambda),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{h}(\lambda)\big)
. Then
\tilde{s}
is also solution of the above system, so
\tilde{s}
and
\hat{s}
coincide on
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d}\setminus\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{Z}^{*}
by Lemma
B.1
. Hence
\tilde{s}
is an extension of
\hat{s}
on
\mathbb{C}_{\Re>d-1}\setminus\mathfrak{P}
, that we will still denote
\hat{s}
. Lemma
B.1
implies that this extension is meromorphic with simple poles and:
\forall q\in\mathfrak{P},\;\operatorname{Res}_{q}\hat{s}=(\operatorname{Res}_{\lambda=q}A(\lambda)^{-1})\big(\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}(q),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{g}(q),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{h}(q)\big)
.

Step 2 :
Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
b\in(d-1,d_{\mathrm{\min}})
and
k\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
. The residue theorem applied to
\lambda\mapsto e^{\lambda t}\hat{s}(\lambda)
on the rectangle
[b,d+1]\times[-k,k]\subset\mathbb{C}
gives the following equality in
\mathcal{H}^{m}
.

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{i}\pi\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{P}}\operatorname{Res}_{q}(\lambda\mapsto e^{\lambda t}\hat{s}(\lambda))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-k}^{k}e^{(d+1+\mathrm{i}\gamma)t}\hat{s}(d+1+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma-\int_{-k}^{k}e^{(b+\mathrm{i}\gamma)t}\hat{s}(b+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma

\displaystyle+\int_{b}^{d+1}e^{(\beta-\mathrm{i}k)t}\hat{s}(\beta-\mathrm{i}k)\,\mathrm{d}\beta-\int_{b}^{d+1}e^{(\beta+\mathrm{i}k)t}\hat{s}(\beta+\mathrm{i}k)\,\mathrm{d}\beta

Note that when
k\rightarrow\infty
the first integral tends to the inverse Laplace transform of
s
(up to a constant). We will show that the last two integrals tend to 0, by proving the following:

{}\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\qquad\sup_{\beta\in[b,d+1]}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]}\gamma^{n}\|\hat{s}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma<\infty\vskip-5.69054pt

(B.7)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in[b,d+1]
. (
3
) and the expressions of
\tilde{g}
and
\tilde{h}
yield

\displaystyle\!\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]}\!\!\gamma^{n}\|\hat{s}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\mathrm{d}\gamma

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]}\!\!\gamma^{n}\|A(\lambda)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}^{m-2}_{\times},\mathcal{H}^{m})}\Big\|\big({\textstyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{g}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{h}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)}\big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m-2}_{\times}}\mathrm{d}\gamma\hskip-56.9055pt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]}\!\gamma^{n+m+2}\,\Big(\|\hat{f}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{H^{m-2}}+\|\hat{g}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{H^{m-2}}+|\hat{h}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\ +\;\|\hat{s}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma+1)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}+\gamma^{2}\|\hat{s}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma+2)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma

Let us treat the term with
f
, given that the others are similar.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1]}\gamma^{n+m+2}\|\hat{f}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{H^{m-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma

\displaystyle\underset{\mathcal{\text{C.S.}}}{\leqslant}

\displaystyle\|\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1])}\cdot\Big\|\gamma^{n+m+3}\|\hat{f}(\beta+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\|_{H^{m-2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\setminus[-1,1])}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|e^{-\beta t}\partial_{t}^{n+m+3}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},H^{m-2}(0,\Theta))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\{b,d+1\}}\|e^{-\beta^{\prime}t}\partial_{t}^{n+m+3}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R},H^{m-2}(0,\Theta))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\{b,d+1\}}\|f\|_{K^{n+m+3}_{\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R},H^{m-2}(0,\Theta))}

thanks to an interpolation between
b
and
d+1
. We have majorized by a finite constant independent of
\beta
, so the supremum on
\beta
is finite. Thus (
B.7
) is proven.

Therefore:
\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\bigg\|\int_{b}^{d+1}e^{(\beta\pm\mathrm{i}k)t}\hat{s}(\beta\pm\mathrm{i}k)\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\bigg\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\mathrm{d}k\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\!\int_{b}^{d+1}\|\hat{s}(\beta\pm\mathrm{i}k)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{m}}\,\mathrm{d}\beta\,\mathrm{d}k<+\infty
.

So by taking arbitrarily large
k
, we can make the terms
\int_{b}^{d+1}e^{(\beta\pm\mathrm{i}\gamma)t}\hat{s}(\beta\pm\mathrm{i}k)\,\mathrm{d}\beta
tend to 0 in
\mathcal{H}^{m}
. Hence:

\sum_{q\in\mathfrak{P}}\operatorname{Res}_{q}(\lambda\mapsto e^{\lambda t}\hat{s}(\lambda))=\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{(d+1+\mathrm{i}\gamma)t}\hat{s}(d+1+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma-\frac{1}{2\mathrm{i}\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{(b+\mathrm{i}\gamma)t}\hat{s}(b+\mathrm{i}\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}\gamma.

Now, since
\hat{s}
has only simple poles, the terms of the sum are equal to

e^{qt}\operatorname{Res}_{q}\hat{s}=e^{qt}(\operatorname{Res}_{\lambda=q}A(\lambda)^{-1})\big({\textstyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\hat{f}(q),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{g}(q),\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}\tilde{h}(q)}\big)=e^{qt}c_{q}\mathbf{1}_{[0,\Theta]}(\theta)\sin(d\theta)=c_{q}\,\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\phi_{q}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}(t,\theta)

for some constant
c_{q}\in\mathbb{C}
by point 2 of Lemma
B.1
. The first intergral is equal to
s(t)
by inverse Laplace transform (because
s\in K_{d+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{H}^{m})
). We define
s_{\mathrm{r}}(t)
to be equal to the last integral. Thus we get the desired formula, and (
B.7
) shows that
s_{\mathrm{r}}\in K^{\infty}_{b}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{H}^{m})
.

\scriptstyle\Box

Let
S
be the function set in Theorem
2.4
and
(\sigma_{d}(S))_{d\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{N}^{*}}
the coefficients of Definition
3.2
, which vanish for
d
big enough. With the
\sigma_{d}(S)
, the non-variational part of
S
(denoted
S^{\mathcal{A}}
below) can be explicitly computed, so it remains to get an asymptotic expansion of its variational part (denoted
S^{V}
below). In order to apply Proposition
B.1
, we need that
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
belongs to some space
K^{\infty}_{\beta}
(since
\Omega_{1}\setminus B(0,R_{\mathrm{c}})=\Pi\setminus B(0,R_{\mathrm{c}})
,
\chi_{\infty}S^{V}
can be seen as a function defined on
\Pi
, which allows us to consider
\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}
). We will use again the notations
\langle.\rangle
and
T_{\geqslant d}
of Definitions
2.1
and
2.4
.

Proposition B.4:
Let
d\in\mathbb{R}_{-}
,

S^{\mathcal{A}}:=T_{\geqslant d}\Bigg[\left\langle-R_{\partial_{Y}^{2}}\circ\partial_{X|\Lambda}^{2},\;\frac{\mu_{0}}{\mu_{1}}R_{\mathrm{N}}\circ\partial_{Y|\Gamma,Y=0^{+}}\right\rangle\bigg(\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}R_{\Delta}(F^{\infty}_{\Omega})+\frac{1}{\mu_{1}}R_{\partial_{Y}^{2}}(F^{\infty}_{\Lambda})+\fsum_{d\in\frac{\pi}{\Theta}\mathbb{N}^{*}}\sigma_{d}(S)\,\phi_{d}\bigg)\Bigg]\vskip-7.11317pt

in
\mathcal{A}(\Pi)
and
S^{V}:=S-\chi_{\infty}S^{\mathcal{A}}
. There is
d\in\mathbb{R}_{-}
s.t.
S^{V}\in V
and:
\forall m\in\mathbb{N},\;\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi_{\infty}S^{V}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}\in K^{\infty}_{1/2}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{H}^{m})
.

.

Proof:
We consider any
d\in\mathbb{R}_{-}
and we will fix it later. The proof has three steps: showing that
S^{V}\in V
, showing that
S^{V}
is regular w.r.t
t
, and deducing that it is regular w.r.t.
\theta
. For any
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
s.t.
R_{\mathrm{c}}<r_{1}<r_{2}
, we will denote
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
a radial function equal to 0 on
B(0,r_{1})
and 1 on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,r_{2})
(
r_{1},r_{2}
are implicit in this notation).

Step 1:
Let
\varphi:\Pi\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be equal to
T_{\leqslant d}(F^{\infty}_{D})
on
D
for any
D\in\{\Omega,\Lambda\}
, and
F_{\mathrm{v}}=F-\chi_{\infty}\varphi
. If
d<-2
then
(1+r)F_{\mathrm{v}}\in L^{2}(\Omega_{1})
. So the same construction than in Theorem
3.2
shows that for
d
small enough, the problem

{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\;=\;}ll}\operatorname{div}(\mu\nabla S^{V})&f_{1}:=F_{\mathrm{v}}+\chi_{\infty}\varphi-\operatorname{div}\!\big(\mu\nabla(\chi_{\infty}\cdot S^{\mathcal{A}})\big)&\text{ in }\Omega_{1}\setminus(\Gamma\cap\{X>R_{\mathrm{c}}\})\\[1.42262pt]
[\mu\partial_{Y}S^{V}]_{\Gamma}&g_{1}:=-[\mu\partial_{Y}(\chi_{\infty}S^{\mathcal{A}})]_{\Gamma}&\text{ on }\Gamma\cap\{X>R_{\mathrm{c}}\}\\[1.42262pt]
S^{V}&0&\text{ on }\partial\Omega_{1}\end{array}\right.

(B.8)

has a unique solution in
V
and that
S=S^{V}+\chi_{\infty}S^{\mathcal{A}}
. In addition, it also gives that
(\mu\Delta S^{\mathcal{A}}-\varphi)_{|D}\in\sum_{d^{\prime}<d}\mathcal{A}_{d^{\prime}}(D)
for any
D\in\{\Omega,\Lambda\}
, and
[\mu\partial_{Y}S^{\mathcal{A}}]_{\Gamma}\in\sum_{d^{\prime}<d}\mathcal{A}_{d^{\prime}}(\Gamma)
. And we have
F_{\mathrm{v}}=o_{\partial}(\mathbf{r}^{d})
by hypothesis, so for
d
small enough and up to increasing
R_{\mathrm{c}}
(without loss of generality):
\forall m\in\mathbb{N},

{}(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi f_{1}$}\mkern-3.0mu}$}}}\mkern 3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-2.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\scriptstyle\chi f_{1}$}\mkern-2.0mu}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu}{\mkern K^{\infty}_{-2}(\mathbb{R},H^{m}(0,\Theta))\quad\text{ and }\quad(\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu{\overbracket{\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\overbracket{\mkern-3.0mu\raisebox{0.0pt}[0.0pt]{$\textstyle\chi (B.9)

and

. And we define
